![](https://i.ormos.uk/6a/6b/6a6bb2655933400e2107e8ec99a85a1db61fa53973d3794357b66e832bedc594.jpg)

Over 620 players showed up to compete at the beginning of the weekend, but only one of them hoisted the championship trophy at the end of the day. That man’s name? Gerry Thompson. No stranger to Grand Prix Top 8s, Thompson was the highest finishing player using a Quick ‘N Toast build inspired by Patrick Chapin (the deck could also be seen in the hands of fellow Top 8er Antonino De Rosa). He bested 18 opponents over the course of the weekend and faced some stiff competition.

The story of the weekend was the plethora of Kithkin decks sitting at the top of the tables at the end of Day 1, but the beatdown deck had just one representative in the Top 8 in the hands of Nate Elkins. He fell to the eventual champion in the semifinals. And Faeries? A whole host of players opted to play the deck on the weekend, but in the end they just didn’t have what it took.

One of the players who opted to play the little fliers was Lee Steht. A popular **Magic** podcaster Steht was one of two Faeries players in the Top 8, but unfortunately the blue-black horde’s fortunes ran out in the finals. Going to an epic third game, Steht came up a tiny bit short paving the way for Gerry Thompson to be crowned Grand Prix-Denver Champion!

## 8:55 am - Day 2 Metagame Breakdown

*by Nate Price*

Here’s the metagame breakdown for Day two of Grand Prix – Denver:

Faeries – 20  
 Kithkin – 12  
 Doran – 9  
 Quick ‘N Toast with [Archon of Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archon+of+Justice) – 5  
 Quick ‘N Toast – 2  
 Quick ‘N Toast with black – 1  
 Merfolk with [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) – 3  
 Merfolk – 2  
 Red – 5  
 RGB Aggro Control (Zero Variance) – 2  
 UB Control (Vitamin King) – 1  
 Elementals – 2

It appears that once again, Faeries is the big deck of the tournament, comprising almost one-third of the Day two decks. The blue fliers have been the top deck in both Block Constructed and Standard pretty much since *Lorwyn* came out. While decks have emerged that have been giving it a run for its money, the Faerie deck still remains the most popular deck in both formats.

![Figure of Destiny](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Figure+of+Destiny)

Biting on the Faeries’ tiny, flying heels is Kithkin. Again, this is no real surprise, since the deck has been one of the more popular decks in Block Constructed since people started testing it. With the addition of [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny), the deck gains an incredible threat in any phase of the game. What is a fairly big surprise is the strength the deck has shown. Faeries have spread themselves out throughout all the tables, but Kithkin has seeded itself mostly at the top tables. It’s been incredibly strong for those players willing to commit to the BESM crew; in fact, the two undefeated players at the start of Day two are both running Kithkin.

Somewhat surprisingly, at least to me, was the prevalence of Doran decks. The incredibly powerful Treefolk and his kin are the third-most played deck in the field on Sunday. Supported by a host of Shaman and aggressive Elves, Doran is the midrange beatdown deck in the format. Thanks to [Wren’s Run Vanquisher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wren%E2%80%99s+Run+Vanquisher) and [Wolf-Skull Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wolf-Skull+Shaman), it can generate an early board presence, but it really starts to take over the game when Doran or [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) hit the table. With [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) and [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) to provide a small amount of supporting control and [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command) providing the big finish, the deck has the raw power needed to push through many opponents’ decks.

Picking a Quick ‘N Toast deck is like picking a flavor of ice cream—there are a million to choose from, but at the end of the day, they’re all just ice cream. More than anything else, Toast has just come to encompass any deck with a $200 mana base. If your deck has the option to play [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher), or [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) on turn six, I’m going to call it Toast. There are two variations in addition to the base version of the deck (as I understand it) that are playing here in the field of sixty-four.

 

![Archon of Justice](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Archon+of+Justice)

First, and the most represented, is the Patrick Chapin-inspired version featuring [Archon of Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archon+of+Justice). The Archon is a versatile way to deal with many cards that give the Toast deck trouble. It kills [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus); [Doran, the Siege Tower](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doran%2C+the+Siege+Tower); and [Oona, Queen of the Fae](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oona%2C+Queen+of+the+Fae). Even better, it removes permanents from the game, allowing it to completely remove a [Demigod of Revenge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Demigod+of+Revenge) from the equation. Since it removes permanents, and not just creatures, it can hit a variety of other threats, like [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom). It’s a little slow, and sometimes you have to kill it yourself, but the effect it generates is usually worth it.

The other variation is the ‘Blackened Toast’ version built by Kyle Bundgaard. It features [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command) as a creature removal option, while simultaneously providing a way to return the many creatures in the deck with comes-into-play effects. It’s also running [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers) which, against a primarily Kithkin and Faerie field, can absolutely demolish the board.

As the day progresses, I’m going to try and check in with some of the designers of the other unique decks to learn a little about their creations and how they have fared for their pilots so far in the tournament.

  

## Sunday, 9:30 a.m.: Day 1 Undefeated Decklists

*by Bill Stark*

Only two players managed to navigate Saturday’s field of 625 completely unscathed. Jarvis Yu and Walter Shatford were the only undefeated players after nine rounds of play, and both made it to that position with their Kithkin decks. Considering both players had traveled from long distances to play (Yu from Maryland and Shatford from Southern California), they’ve got to be pretty pleased to wake up on Sunday within striking distance of the Top 8.

But can they keep the momentum going? You’ll have to check back and see throughout the day!

## Feature Match Round 10 – Walter Shatford vs. Jarvis Yu

*by Bill Stark*

"So, you had three byes huh?" Jarvis Yu asked his opponent as he sat to shuffle, one of only two undefeated players at the end of the first day of competition.

"Yeah. How many did you have?" The second undefeated player and his opponent for the round, Walter Shatford, asked.

"Just two," sighed Yu.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/16/90/16907ec8babe96f6fe34dd9a122da525a67652ccff5134201e49fcc6e9c04d75.jpg)*Only two byes for 9-0 Jarvis Yu.*Jarvis managed to win the die roll and kept his opening hand. He came blistering out of the gates with a first turn [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart), but his opponent matched the play with a first-turn Stalwart of his own. Not backing down Yu continued play with a second Stalwart and a [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny).

To stay in it, Walter Shatford had a [Knight of Meadowgrain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Meadowgrain), but the singleton 2/2 body paled in comparison to Jarvis’ [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn) with mana up to make a level one transformation of his [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny). When he cracked in he did exactly that, smacking Walter for 9. That left the totals 18-9 in Jarvis’ favor, but Shatford wasn’t backing down, bashing right back with his [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart) to make things 16-9. He didn’t have any more creatures to add to the board, however, passing the turn.

The game was simply whizzing by, with both players, clearly familiar with the nuances of their Kithkin builds, sending the turns back and forth quickly. Shatford killed a free [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart) with his Knight of the Meadowgrain and a +1/+1 counter from [Rustic Clachan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rustic+Clachan). He still fell to 4 on the attack when Jarvis Yu level twoed his Figure into a 5/5 thanks to [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn).

Jarvis’ [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake) dealt with his opponent’s Knight, and Walter was forced to use a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart) in an effort to stay alive through an attack. That left him at 1, however, and when Jarvis cracked back with three creatures the following turn, Walter considered his options before being forced to use a [Mirrorweave](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mirrorweave) targeting [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault). That turned all creatures into [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s, functioning as a Fog for the turn, but when Shatford found no help for him on the top of his deck he packed it in.

Yu 1, Shatford 0.

"So where you from?" Jarvis Yu asked his opponent while shuffling for the second game.

"SoCal," Walter Shatford replied, quietly. "How ‘bout you?"

Smiling Yu said "Maryland. So I guess we both traveled a long ways to be here."

On the play for the second game, Walter Shatford quickly sent his opening hand back, but, happy with his set of six started on a [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart). In a mirror of their first game, Jarvis Yu followed his opponent’s lead with an opening 2/2 himself, revealing a [Knight of Meadowgrain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Meadowgrain). Walter, however, had no second-turn play making a [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights) instead.

By the time the third turn had rolled around it was Jarvis’ turn to miss a play with no creatures or other spells available. Shatford was all too happy to take advantage of that, activating his [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights) to put Ajani into play after he had attacked with three [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) Spirits and a [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart). Jarvis was backpedaling with the totals at 16-8, but he managed to use an attack to deal with Ajani and some [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake)s to deal with Walter’s biggest threats. Yu’s Knight of the Meadowgrain helped keep him in it as well, picking up a +1/+1 counter from [Rustic Clachan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rustic+Clachan), and disrupting his opponent’s clock through lifelink. When Walter revealed an [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake) of his own for the first striker, however, Yu had no choice but to concede.

Yu 1, Shatford 1.

For the third time in the match, Jarvis Yu had a [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart) to kick off the game, and was soon staring down a Walter Shatford [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny). Yu, apparently a fan of the "1/1" himself, was happy to spend his second turn playing two of them. Then things got really out of hand.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/cb/dd/cbddd7a523138fafd85f46f52d0cbaef1524663b5941c933ff0dd29957cf8e01.jpg)*How would Walter Shatford fare in a mirror match?*Shatford could only make a [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn) to build his board while Yu unloaded with a [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) and [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn) of his own, filling the board with his creatures and leaving up the possibility of turning his Figures of Destiny into 3/3s with a pump and the Cenn. Shatford was definitely on his back foot and needed to find help quick. The all-out attack from Jarvis Yu took Shatford to 6, though he did manage to eat one of his opponent’s Figures with his own plus some help from [Rustic Clachan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rustic+Clachan).

A second all-in attack from Yu forced Walter’s hand, Unmaking Yu’s [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny) before damage but still falling to just 3 life. Facing down three [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) Spirits without a flier in sight, Shatford needed some help from the top of his deck to stay alive. He found it in the form of [Ajani Goldmane](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Goldmane), but because of his low life total was forced to use the planeswalker to gain 2 life. The totals stood 17-2 after Jarvis Yu’s next attack and Shatford, at a virtual 4 thanks to Ajani, had, for the moment, stabilized.

A [Cloudgoat Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudgoat+Ranger) helped Shatford’s cause, offering him a blocker and buying him a little bit more time to defeat Jarvis. When Yu made his next attack with the three Spirits, however, he revealed two [Rustic Clachan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rustic+Clachan)s on the two fliers Shatford was forced to leave unblocked, enough to deal exactly lethal just as his opponent was taking control of the game.

Jarvis Yu defeats Walter Shatford 2-1.

  

## Round 11 Feature Match: Making Waves - Kyle Bundgaard vs. Tyrell Wheeler

*by Nate Price*

Both players kept their opening draws, and Bundgaard started the game off with a quick [Vivid Creek](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Creek). Wheeler got things rolling quickly with a second-turn [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom). When his first [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) token came into play, he tried searching for a pen to alter his life total. As he dug through his pockets, a pen came flying like a rocket from the crowd behind him. It skittered across the table and right into Bundgaard's lap. All three of us turned to look at where it had come from, and the player that launched it sheepishly said, "Sorry. I thought he needed a pen." In his hand, not sticking out of the back of his head. Good looking out, though.

Bundgaard tried to play a [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers) on his fifth turn, and when it hit, it killed the two tokens Wheeler had built up. He immediately retaliated by killing the Snuffers with a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion). Buungard just shrugged and put it into the graveyard. Wheeler kept making tokens with his [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom), but, as of yet, it had dealt more to him than his tokens had done to Bundgaard. Kyle evoked a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) on his turn to draw a few cards, and passed the turn. Wheeler was pleased enough to just attack with his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and Faerie tokens.

When he tried on his next turn to continue to do just that, Bundgaard attempted to [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) his Snuffers back, which would have allowed him to wipe the slate. Unfortunately for him, Wheeler was prepared with a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) to counter it. Bundgaard got a chance to untap and play another, though, wiping Wheeler's team clear. Wheeler kept on swinging with his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), and when Bundgaard blocked with his Snuffers, a [Peppersmoke](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peppersmoke) made sure that it was the only thing that died.

Bundgaard tried a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) on the following turn, which would put him back up to fourteen, but Wheeler was prepared with a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite). Bundgaard was behind in life, and on the board, but he had a handful of cards, whereas Wheeler was down to his last two. "They're good, though," Wheeler warned Bundgaard. "I expect they would be; Faeries doesn't usually run bad cards."

After his [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) was countered, Bundgaard tried to [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) the board clear again, but Wheeler had a second [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) to stop it, leaving him on one card. His following attack dropped Bundgaard to seven. When he tried to play a [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona) on the following attack to kill Bundgaard, Kyle was ready with a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) to return [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers), wiping Wheeeler's board clear again.

"I guess I got greedy there," Wheeler sighed as he put his Sprites into the graveyard. "Got a little greedy."

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f6/94/f69499099fca781f83e69b9e02e1bd3eefe3b6511abd6355c8747ed12ba32f65.jpg)*Wheeler finds that sometimes, getting greedy pays.*  
 Bundgaard had a Faerie of his own on the following turn. [Oona, Queen of the Fae](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oona%2C+Queen+of+the+Fae) was batting for the other team now. In response to Oona, Wheeler played a [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) to tap Bundgaard's last land and get rid of his own [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom). Wheeler drew his card and lit up a little. "That's a good one!"

"[Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation)," Bundgaard ventured?

"No, not that good."

[Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), returning Oona and drawing a card, while not as good as Sower, was still good enough to let the [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) attack Bundgaard down to three. When Bundgaard tried to [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command) the [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) and return a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to play, Wheeler revealed that his one card in hand, the one he had Cryptic commanded into on the previous turn, was another [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command). "Command into Command," Wheeler sympathetically offered. Bundgaard just shot him a depressed look and passed the turn, tapped out and with nothing to do. All Wheeler had to do was untap his men and attack, and they were off to Game 2.

Wheeler 1, Bundgaard 0

Paul Cheon and some of the other Colorado players were waiting in the background and cheering Bundgaard on. "Come one, guys, let's do the wave." As the four-man wave went down and back the line, Cheon remarked, "You can't lose now, Kyle! We just did the wave for you. Twice!"

"Where's my support," Wheeler wondered as he looked behind Bundgaard.

"You're from Vancouver," Bundgaard reminded him, "The drive's a little farther."

Despite the tremendous amount of support Bundgaard was receiving, his deck neglected to give him seven playable cards. He managed to talk his deck into giving him six playable, though, and wasn't too disappointed when Wheeler dropped to six as well.

This game started in similar fashion the first game, with one notable difference. Bundgaard dropped a couple of Vivid lands into play while Wheeler [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder)ed a couple of times. Instead of [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) on the second turn, though, Wheeler [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize)d Bundgaard, taking a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) out of a hand that also contained two Kitchen finks and a [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout). One of the Finks came down on the following turn, ready to assault Wheeler. Wheeler simply played his land and passed the turn. During Bundgaard's draw, he sprang into action, playing a [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique), which washed away another [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from a hand that had gained a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) as well. The [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) kept beating, and a second copy soon joined them. Wheeler was down to fourteen.

Wheeler played a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom), and the tokens slowly started to spew forth. Bundgaard evoked a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw), killing the [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique). That let him attack Wheeler down to five. Wheeler was forced to chump block one of the Finks, which dropped him to eleven. When Bundgaard attacked on the following turn, Wheeler activated his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and tried to block. Before he could declare his blockers, though, Bundgaard attempted to [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) the [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) back. Wheeler couldn't allow this, though, and had to tap his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) to [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) it, countering the spell and drawing a card.

The next turn went a little better for Wheeler, as he was able to [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) one of the Finks, and block the other one with his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault). They just came back, but they were now vulnerable to [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers), as well as easily killed by [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) tokens. Bundgaard attempted to [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) for fliers only on the following turn in an attempt to force Wheeler to block with his lone remaining [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), but he had a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite), bolstering his forces and forcing Bundgaard to hold his team back.

 

![Bitterblossom](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Bitterblossom)

With Bundgaard now on the defensive and [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) dropping him to three, Wheeler had to make something happen and chose to attack with his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault). Bundgaard just blocked with his now neutered [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks). An attempted [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers) met a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) from Wheeler. He had an impressive flying army now, but Bundgaard was at a huge life total, and [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) would drop him to one during his next upkeep. Before drawing his card, Wheeler pleaded with his deck, "C'mon, one time!"

With a rather sullen look, he just attacked and played a [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona) to drop Bundgaard to a little less than infinity. He passed the turn to Bundgaard, and then offered him some play advice. "[Wispmare](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wispmare)," he asked? Bundgaard just smiled and passed the turn back. During his upkeep, [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) finished Wheeler off.

Bundgaard 1, Wheeler 1

Wheeler started the final game off exactly as he would like. Turn-two [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) is always a stellar start for a Faerie deck. Bundgaard started with a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) that almost immediately met a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) from Wheeler. A Faerie token finished the Finks off on the ensuing attack, leaving Wheeler with the only board presence.

Bundgaard evoked a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter), figuring that he needed the cards in the long run. During his next upkeep, Wheeler attempted a [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique), which prompted a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from Bundgaard. Wheeler came prepared, though, and had a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) that not only countered the Command, but served as champion bait for the Clique. Wheeler now had an impressive team, but Bundgaard was still at twenty. Wheeler's team knocked that to thirteen before passing the turn. He had a [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) during Bundgaard's draw, which forced Bundgaard to evoke a [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) in response, killing all but Wheeler's [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique).

After the Thresher did his work, Wheeler was treated to a hand containing two [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command)s, a [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command), [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw), Oona, [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion), and [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin). "Jeez, you've got a good hand." Wheeler decided to force Bundgaard to exchange a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) for something new. After drawing his replacement, Bundgaard evoked a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) to kill the [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique), demoting it to [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) status.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9e/27/9e27360c75fa2a8993d9c1ae6e9b85dd87e633b722476ee2817b301443de22bd.jpg)*Even Bundgaard is enthralled by how good his hand is.*  
 The Sprite knocked Bundgaard to ten, but Wheeler didn't have anything else. When Bundgaard played a [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers) on the following turn, all Wheeler could do was mope, "Yep. Wow, you had a good hand." Wheeler found a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), but was forced to trade it in for the [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers) on the following turn. When Wheeler started to attack with his [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) tokens on the following turn, Bundgaard had a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) for his [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers), wiping the board clear yet again.

Wheeler had a [Snakeform](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snakeform) to kill the Snuffers, but Bundgaard just upgraded it to an [Oona, Queen of the Fae](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oona%2C+Queen+of+the+Fae). Wheeler drew his card for the turn, and then flipped it over targeting Oona. [Eyeblight's Ending](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eyeblight%27s+Ending). "You just ripped that? Pretty nice." Bundgaard dropped a fully-cast [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) into play on the following turn. When Wheeler continued to offer no resistance, Bundgaard played [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command) to return Oona to play and drop Wheeler to two. He could have gotten back a [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) to outright kill Wheeler, which Wheeler immediately pointed out.

Kyle Bundgaard defeats Tyrell Wheeler 2-1

  

## Round 12 Feature Match: Zach Thompson vs. Antonino De Rosa

*by Bill Stark*

"Ft. Collins," was how Zach Thompson answered his opponent’s question of "where are you from?" as he sat down for his feature match. Sitting across from him was **Magic** pro and long-time player Antonino De Rosa. The Italian transplant has been a resident of the United States for years and once managed back-to-back-to-back Top 8s at the U.S. Nationals tournament, including a stint as its champion. Both players entered the round at 8-2.

After winning the opportunity to play first, Thompson opened on a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) but didn’t seem to be playing Faeries, instead running a manabase more akin to a Doran deck. Antonino, meanwhile, built up his five-color Quick ‘N Toast manabase using a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) to counter his opponent’s [Scarblade Elite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scarblade+Elite).

"I wish the clash on that card was a ‘may’ ability. I’ve only seen people lose when they win the clash." De Rosa opined.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3c/c6/3cc6a35dd2496150d10b56533d2d38e9213a96ff7b1b1b3d3bcd0f90d6b1f9cc.jpg)*Zach Thompson gives it his all against a pro.*Thompson’s [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) was contained with a [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout), allowing De Rosa to build up his manabase and cards in hand thanks to [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter)s. When Zach played a [Doran, the Siege Tower](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doran%2C+the+Siege+Tower), however, the American stopped evoking [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter)s and hard-cast one of them, possibly hoping to chump block. The Colorado native Thompson put an end to that possibility with a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) on the 2/2. De Rosa didn’t seem concerned, and when he cast an [Austere Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Austere+Command) setting the modes on ‘destroy enchantments’ and ‘destroy creatures with casting cost three or less’ he wiped Thompson’s board entirely.

Not one to go down without a fight, Zach simply played a second [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom). He was quickly falling behind to Antonino’s massive card draw machine and an [Archon of Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archon+of+Justice) that threatened the stability of Zach’s token-generating enchantment. When a second [Archon of Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archon+of+Justice) joined the table followed by yet another [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter), Thompson was quickly down for the count.

De Rosa 1, Thompson 0.

"Everyone’s playing these lame Kithkin and Faeries decks," De Rosa teased the feature match next to him while shuffling for his second game. In said feature match Paulo Vitor Dama Da Rosa and his opponent Jarvis Yu, incidentally playing Faeries and Kithkin decks, didn’t seem to pay the good natured jibing any mind.

Both players kicked off the second game with a mulligan, but were happy with their sixes. Thompson used a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) to nab his opponent’s [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter), and thought he had managed to clear the way for a [Fulminator Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fulminator+Mage) to resolve. He was shocked to find his opponent had ripped a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) from the top of his library, countering the 2/2. A second copy of the Elemental Shaman resolved, however, and De Rosa was forced to place a [Vivid Creek](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vivid+Creek) in the graveyard.

Still, the plucky Italian-American managed to make his land drops, using a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) to force Thompson to regret selecting the [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) with his [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) as the 2/2 returned to play and juiced Antonino’s hand. A [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) kept De Rosa’s life total high in the face of Zach’s [Wren’s Run Vanquisher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wren%E2%80%99s+Run+Vanquisher) and [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus), and a [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial) let Antonino clear Thompson’s board.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/8d/84/8d84192983996469d77c3f5d83aab0cee6a57cea4c467a398e1734c76016a667.jpg)*Antonino De Rosa is not afraid of the feature match area.*Zach tried to get back into control of the game by unleashing monsters adding [Scarblade Elite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scarblade+Elite), [Fulminator Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fulminator+Mage), and [Doran, the Siege Tower](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doran%2C+the+Siege+Tower) to the board. Antonino didn’t seem fazed, however, as his [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) and massive accumulation of mana were very close to lethal against his opponent’s board of all black creatures. Thompson absorbed one attack from the Colossus falling to 9 before finding a [Wren’s Run Vanquisher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wren%E2%80%99s+Run+Vanquisher) to stymie the Colossus.

Trying to find a way to maneuver in for a win Thompson went for a [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command) off the top, threatening an attack with an all-fear force while sending 4 damage straight to Ant’s head. De Rosa simply revealed a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) to prevent that from happening, then used a [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial) to reset both player’s boards, and followed up with a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter). Thompson could only play from the top of his deck, but was happy to find a Doran waiting for him there. Unfortunately for Zach, the 0/5 met an untimely demise at the hands of a surprise [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) block. When De Rosa had a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to eliminate his opponent’s chump blocker, the ‘Thresher was able to swing for the match.

Antonino De Rosa defeats Zach Thompson 2-0.

  

## 11:53 a.m. - Deck Tech with Conley Woods

*by Nate Price*

Every time I look through the decklists to prepare the Day two metagame breakdown, I try to keep an eye out for interesting decks near the top of the standings. Conley Woods, a student at Colorado State University in nearby Fort Collins, is playing a pretty slick UB control number featuring a bunch of interesting card choices.

Temptation";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}i:17;O:8:"stdClass":3:{s:4:"type";s:7:"instant";s:10:"deck\_count";i:0;s:9:"card\_meta";O:8:"stdClass":2:{s:5:"title";s:6:"Unmake";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}i:18;O:8:"stdClass":3:{s:4:"type";s:8:"creature";s:10:"deck\_count";i:0;s:9:"card\_meta";O:8:"stdClass":2:{s:5:"title";s:11:"Festercreep";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}i:19;O:8:"stdClass":3:{s:4:"type";s:8:"creature";s:10:"deck\_count";i:0;s:9:"card\_meta";O:8:"stdClass":2:{s:5:"title";s:22:"Oona, Queen of the Fae";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}i:20;O:8:"stdClass":3:{s:4:"type";s:7:"sorcery";s:10:"deck\_count";i:0;s:9:"card\_meta";O:8:"stdClass":2:{s:5:"title";s:11:"Syphon Life";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

"The deck is essentially a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) deck," Conley explained to me as he laid his deck out in front of me. The deck features [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw), [Puppeteer Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer+Clique), [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers), and [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) as potential Mannequin targets, each of which fills a very niche role in the deck. Conley explained that his deck is built to have a certain level of inevitability. It uses early evoked [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw)s, [Snakeform](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snakeform), and [Raven's Crime](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raven%27s+Crime) to slow opponents down enough to reach the later stages of the game where the [River Kelpie](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=River+Kelpie) engine can start to take over.

![Makeshift Mannequin](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Makeshift+Mannequin)

The most important matchup for Conley to consider was the inevitable Faeries match. In theory, the early turns of the Faeries match aren't that aggressive, so there isn't much to kill. This allows [Raven's Crime](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raven%27s+Crime) to go to work on their hand. Conley said he'd be happy to discard a bunch of lands early on in the game to strip away their hand. His reasoning behind this is that if the Faeries deck is forced to play from the top of their deck, their draws become very unimpressive. [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona) and [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) are pretty ineffective if you don't already have the advantage somehow.

[Raven's Crime](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raven%27s+Crime) also strips through their countermagic. With most Faeries decks running some combination of [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions), and [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite), they have a lot of permission to fight around. Conley takes a different approach. "You don't worry about fighting around their counterspells, you just play spells and raw them out." If they are wasting their time and mana countering your early spells, they won't be able to stop you when it matters most. You can win a game from two or three life, especially since the Faeries deck has to kill you through combat, which is impossible with no attackers.

![Raven's Crime](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Raven%27s+Crime)

[Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers) and [Puppeteer Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer+Clique) are also all-stars against the Fae. Snuffers are pretty much a Wrath of God against the many flying 1/1s, and [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) can make it come at instant speed. The [Puppeteer Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer+Clique) is a little more insidious, especially combined with the Mannequin. It can steal a [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) during their upkeep, and effectively use their own tricks against them. In addition, it's a flier with persist, which can buy you much needed time if they've managed to get the advantage over you.

After sideboarding, you get to take out your fairly ineffective [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw)s and [Snakeform](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snakeform)s to bring in [Festercreep](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Festercreep)s, [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), and [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake). This allows even more control of the board, as well as the ability to strip the [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique)s before they become an issue. If it's at a later stage of the game, the [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize)s strip the [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command)s out and clear the way for more powerful effects.

According to Conley, the other aggressive matches, like Kithkin and Merfolk, go much the same way. The only real difference in those is that they have a greater proclivity for playing early creatures. Woods said that he had no problem blowing an early [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) or [Snakeform](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snakeform) just to buy himself a few extra turns. Eventually, he would get to do some Soul Snuffer or [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) tricks with [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin), and he'll be back in control. It's inevitable.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6a/25/6a259435c56004a378883ef03a3588d1dd89531116134b5a714d2a15ecdd5073.jpg)*Conley Woods hopes to beat the format black and blue.*

In general, Conley feels really strongly about his deck's choice for this weekend. "This is a really metagamed deck. The whole deck is geared to beat Faeries and Kithkin, while still having enough versatility to take any comers. The sideboard provides additional ways to control the creature population, as well as [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize)s for the control matches. In all, the only real change that he would make in the deck is the lone [Syphon Life](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Syphon+Life) in the board. It was originally intended to help beat the red decks, but it wasn't very effective at that job, and he hasn't really played against any red this weekend, so it hasn't mattered.

  

## Round 13 Feature Match - Nice and Dramatic - Hunter Coale vs. Conley Woods

*by Nate Price*

Woods won the die roll, and chose to go first. "Ugh," he groaned as he peeked at his starting seven. His hand had two [Snakeform](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snakeform)s and five lands. Woods didn't even have to think before tossing it back for a much better six. Coale, unfortunately, was forced to drop all the way to five. "That's no bueno," Woods sympathized.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3f/08/3f087bba85dca3a3f5ffcefc4e02f358f6cd7a1f1c92af6f7d23fe16db56c81c.jpg)*All Coale needs is two lands and a Bitterblossom.*  
 Woods made matters worse with first- and second- turn [Raven's Crime](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raven%27s+Crime)s. Coale just discarded a couple of lands and played a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) on his second turn. That's all you really need to get right into a game, regardless of your starting hand size. He also had two [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s, which would allow him to be very aggressive.

On the other side of the table, Woods, who had started fairly strong, had failed to find a fourth land, and was forced to cycle through a [Snakeform](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snakeform) in an attempt to find one. Despite discarding two early lands, Coale had managed to find a few more off the top of his deck, and always seemed to have [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) mana up. Woods needed to find a way to slow the bleeding, and he had to do it through a potential counterspell.

He managed to get a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to tap all of Coale's men and draw himself a card, buying himself another turn of life. However, when he tried to play a [River Kelpie](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=River+Kelpie), it met a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions). Woods was unable to come up with anything else to stop the beats and had to pack it up in the face of Coale's pretty sick mulligan.

"Nice five-carder," Woods mentioned as he reached for his sideboard.

"Yeah, it was four lands and a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom)," Coale revealed, just proving how powerful and important the start is for Faeries. He played only one spell other than [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) that game, and, to be honest, he may not have even needed to in order to win that game.

Coale 1, Woods 0

With the [Festercreep](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Festercreep)s and [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake)s coming in out of the board, Woods was confident that the matchup was in his favor after sideboarding.

Woods explained the previous game by letting me know that he "wanted to make it dramatic" for me. "You can't do a deck tech and then make it a blowout."

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f1/cb/f1cb5d04cde2e6753100a1f22e077a78363018f61f9932c83dfe7223444b0e67.jpg)*This wasn't the kind of drama that Woods was hoping for.*  
 The first exchange of the game came at the end of Woods' fourth turn, an attempted [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) met a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command). Now that Woods was tapped out, Coale took the opportunity to [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) out of Woods' hand of [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers) and [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake). Woods tried to make a [Puppeteer Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer+Clique), but a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) put a stop to that. A couple of turns, Coale tried to make a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom). Woods had a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to stop it, but a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from Coale turned the tables back on Woods. With the [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) in play now, Woods was going to need to take control of the game. He stole a [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) with a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), leaving another one and a [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation) in Coale's hand. Those Faeries, combined with the ticking [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) meant bad times for Woods.

[Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) went to work on both players. Woods dropped to fourteen as Coale swung in with two [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s. A [Raven's Crime](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raven%27s+Crime) from Woods stripped Coale of his Sower, leaving him with just a [Mistbind Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mistbind+Clique) and a mystery card that Woods had to worry about. [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers) then wiped away Coale's tokens, sadly missing the [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique) that came down soon after. The Clique bashed in and dropped Woods to eleven.

An attempted [River Kelpie](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=River+Kelpie) got hit by a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions), which left Coale with one card in hand. A retraced [Raven's Crime](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raven%27s+Crime) knocked it out, and Coale was relegated to the top of his deck. Unfortunately, his top card was a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite), which stopped the [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake) aimed at the [Vendilion Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vendilion+Clique). That left Coale with enough attackers to finish Woods off, giving him his first loss to Faeries on the weekend, and it couldn't have come at a better time for Coale.

Hunter Coale defeats Conley Woods 2-0

  

## 12:57 pm - Deck Tech - Kyle Goodman and Alex Kim

*by Nate Price*

My first thought upon looking at Alex and Kyle's decklist was that it looked like just a random collection of the best cards in the format. It was as though they'd just taken every deck's MVP, put them in with some [Incendiary Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incendiary+Command)s, and called it a deck. After talking with them for a few minutes, it all started to make sense to me.
