

![](https://i.ormos.uk/41/53/415314c08c607969a711b8739da3c0543afc1751afc78aacf8644113c8aa53a2.jpg)*Alex Kim's Ashenmoor Gouger is really big.* The creatures in this deck are all incredible at what they do. [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny) is a wonderfully versatile threat at any stage in the game. [Fulminator Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fulminator+Mage) punishes the fact that every deck seems to be running the greediest mana base since Miracle Gro. [Demigod of Revenge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Demigod+of+Revenge) and [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) may have killed more people than Hannibal (Lecter or the Carthaginian, whichever impresses you more). And [Ashenmoor Gouger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ashenmoor+Gouger), well, he's just really big.

After testing a little against the big decks in the format, Alex and Kyle came to the conclusion that land destruction might be good. "Faeries has such a shaky mana base. It might be the greediest mana base in the format," Kyle let me know as he pointed to the [Fulminator Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fulminator+Mage)s and [Incendiary Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incendiary+Command)s in his deck. His rationale behind that was that they're running [Sunken Ruins](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sunken+Ruins) and [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), even going so far as to run a couple of [Reflecting Pool](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reflecting+Pool)s in some cases. If they can kill the Ruins, and stick the Faeries player with [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s and basic lands, it severely cuts into their ability to play [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), which is their main tool for stabilizing the game.

Another concept behind the deck is the versatility and card advantage inherent in each of the cards. "Each of the cards does multiple things, which gives us a generic sort of card advantage. We figured that [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) was too slow for its effect. Against Faeries and Kithkin, you really can't afford to pay five mana to get a two-for-one. We figured that cheaper card advantage spells, like [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout), were the key to the format," Alex explained.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f2/a6/f2a657971317f557761627093f22851870844246d3eddf7f8601b8ab1ebd5b3c.jpg)*Kyle Goodman ponders his options.*

The versatility is really exemplified in the Faeries matchup. "How we play the game changes completely based on whether or not they play turn two [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom). If they don't, we just play some guys and attack their mana base. If they do, we switch to trying to wipe the board and burn them out. [Incendiary Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incendiary+Command), [Lash Out](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lash+Out), and [Flame Javelin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flame+Javelin) all hopefully end up going to the dome." This effectively turns the [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) into a weapon that can be used against the Faeries player.

A few of the more interesting inclusions in the deck are [Lash Out](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lash+Out) and [Eyeblight's Ending](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eyeblight%27s+Ending). [Lash Out](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lash+Out) was chosen over [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) mostly because of the fact that it accomplishes the same thing, but provides a few extra bonuses. The extra damage from won clashes adds up, not to mention that clashing allows you to filter your draws. The [Eyeblight's Ending](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eyeblight%27s+Ending) has been a real all-star for them this weekend. It has the advantage of being able to kill every major threat in the format except [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) and [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault). Doran, Oona, Demigod, and their ilk all roll over and die to the three mana instant. It's really quite efficient.

 

![Guttural Response](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Guttural+Response)

The card that they've been most pleased with this weekend, though, is in their sideboard. With more players playing [Consign to Dream](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consign+to+Dream) and [Snakeform](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snakeform), [Guttural Response](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Guttural+Response) has been working overtime. Add to that its ability to stop a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), and you've got a real winner. Most decks that run [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) commit so heavily to plays involving it that stopping one can be a huge advantage. The Commands can provide a huge tempo swing, and the Responses allow the aggressive player to stay on the offensive. Since it only costs one mana, you can do a bunch of other stuff after casting it, too. They commit four mana to trying to bounce your [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus), so you just Response it and use your left over mana to pump it. The advantage is huge.

Kyle and Alex admit that they had planned on a metagame similar to the one seen here today. There's a bit less Kithkin than they had planned, but there's still enough Faeries floating around with the little white men that they are happy with their choice. They can wipe the board with either [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) or [Incendiary Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Incendiary+Command) and finish the game pretty much however they choose. They're both sitting at 10-3 going into round 14, needing to win out to have a chance at Top 8. Regardless, though, I'd say their deck choice was just fine for the environment, and has served them well this weekend.

  

## Feature Match Round 14 - Steve Sadin vs. Aaron Gunzner

*by Bill Stark*

"I certainly know I'm not famous," said Aaron Gunzner as he sat down to face Steve Sadin. "Are you?"

Smiling the New Yorker Sadin just shrugged. "I travel to a lot of tournaments and I write," he said before handing his deck to his opponent to be shuffled.

Gunzner, a Colorado native, brought an aggressive red deck to the table and made no qualms about immediately getting into the red zone with a [Tattermunge Maniac](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tattermunge+Maniac) and [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny). Sadin slowed the onslaught by using an evoked [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) to kill a [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny) and a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) to make up on some lost life, one of the many American pros running a Patrick Chapin inspired Quick 'N Toast build. Aaron simply started sending [Flame Javelin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flame+Javelin)s at Steve's head, quickly leaving the totals at 18-8 in Gunzner's favor.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/cd/80/cd80a4ba1055b5fbdf6f4a5c9f814b0224b2232aae1b1ff2630e0859c4a6ce14.jpg)*Aaron Gunzner brings the burn.*Things stayed like that for some time, with Sadin going on the offensive with a 2/1 [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks). Gunzner had entered every red mage's worst nightmare: the mid-game. Lands started peeling off the top of his library while he desperately sought more burn spells to try to push through the last few points of damage. When he finally found a [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny), Steve was only too happy to use a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) to return a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) and kill the 1/1.

Aaron Gunzner was out of gas, and Steve Sadin was beginning to take advantage of that fact, using a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to bounce his [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) and draw a card, de facto countering Aaron's [Lash Out](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lash+Out) targeting the 2/1. Steve was so confident on his life total, in fact, that he risked a full quarter of it by making a surprise [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher). When Aaron couldn't find an answer to the 7/7, the players were on to Game 2.

Sadin 1, Gunzner 0

[Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny) and [Stigma Lasher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stigma+Lasher) were the first two plays for Aaron Gunzner in the second game of his feature match. When the Lasher managed to sneak in for 2 damage, any hopes Steve Sadin had of regaining lost life momentum on the back of cards like [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) went out the window. He did have a [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) to answer Aaron's [Ashenmoor Gouger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ashenmoor+Gouger), even winning the clash.

Gunzner kept coming, however, trading [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny) for his opponent's surprise [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) and adding a [Boggart Ram-Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boggart+Ram-Gang) to the field of play, making the score 20-4 in his favor. Sadin's [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial) put an end to the creature attack but, tapped out, Steve could only watch helplessly as his opponent played a [Rekindled Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rekindled+Flame) for the final points of his life total.

Sadin 1, Gunzner 1.

Aaron Gunzner's third game mulligan was offset to some extent by the fact he was on the draw. His [Tattermunge Maniac](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tattermunge+Maniac), however, was "dealt with" as Steve Sadin used a [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) to give himself protection from the 2/1. Unfortunately for him, Gunzner's second-turn [Stigma Lasher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stigma+Lasher)*did* manage to get in, again shutting off Steve's lifegain.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7a/b3/7ab325f05122c352c19f65d942ecde8c73fdb98a124f5a8608197d54dc9620a1.jpg)*Steve Sadin tries to keep his head above water.*Sadin's opponent was keeping things fair by missing land drops, stuck on only two. When he finally hit his third to play [Boggart Ram-Gang](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Boggart+Ram-Gang), Sadin was quick to [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) the 3/3 and draw himself a free card. He had built his manabase up to six lands, but had only played the [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) and [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) for spells. When Gunzner tried to repeat his Ram-Gang play, Steve obliged by making a second [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command). He then found a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) to answer his opponent's [Stigma Lasher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stigma+Lasher).

The life totals stood at 20-9 in Aaron's favor, and he worked diligently to keep things that way. When he hit a fourth land he played a [Spiteful Visions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spiteful+Visions), offering Steve a Howling Mine but at the cost of 2 damage a turn. Sadin had no choice but to play a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) and take 2 more damage from the Visions in an effort to find a solution to his opponent's Enchantment, but he got it in the form of a second [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo). Unfortunately that left him at just 3 life, and when Aaron revealed a [Demigod of Revenge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Demigod+of+Revenge), Sadin graciously extended his hand in defeat.

Aaron Gunzner defeats Steve Sadin 2-1.

After the match Steve revealed he had completely misinterpreted his opening hand. He had mistakenly thought he'd had a source of black mana which would have allowed him to evoke his [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) early. The costly mistake leaves him hoping for a shot at Top 16 and/or Top 32 while his opponent stays within range of the Top 8.

  

## Sunday, 2:16 p.m.: Public Events

*by Bill Stark*

One of the best things about premier events like Pro Tours and Grand Prixs is the fact that you don’t have to be a pro to have an absolute blast. All weekend long at Grand Prix-Denver hundreds of players have enjoyed the numerous things there are to do outside of playing the Grand Prix. And of course, once you’ve had your fill of the main event, chatting with guest artist (and Mox illustrator extraordinaire) Dan Frazier, and perusing the numerous card dealers, there are the public events.

Grand Prix- Denver certainly hasn’t disappointed in that arena. Over 200 players showed up for the Pro Tour Qualifier today, hoping to earn a ticket to the upcoming Pro Tour- Berlin. They’re busy battling the exact same format as the participants of Day 2 at the Grand Prix: *Lorwyn/Shadowmoor* Block Constructed. As they enter the middle of the tournament, the players are sure to have a long day ahead of them.

One of the more popular public events Saturday was the Sealed Deck for a Nintendo Wii. Russell Harris walked away the winner there, earning the nifty video game system. He managed to do it despite earning a game loss early in the event for tardiness, which just goes to show even if things don’t seem to be going your way tightening up and focusing can make all the difference. Or, at least a *wii* bit of difference...

On top of those events are a trial awarding byes for the upcoming Minnesota $5,000 tournament, a Standard event giving away a Mox (how fitting with Dan Frazier only feet away from the event), and of course a Grand Prix Trial for the upcoming Grand Prix- Kansas City, one of the next Grand Prixs in North America.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/90/5f/905fbd9f4bb78697c5fdde3b4b3405dc5fb4db08ec5a2b408cd766f3ba84244e.jpg)*Eight players happy to be public eventing at Grand Prix- Denver.*If none of *those* events tickles your fancy, 8-person Sealed, Draft, and Standard events are constantly firing as they fill up. In fact, as a nod to the increased payout for the Summer Series players involved in the 8-person tournaments at Grand Prix- Denver get a special bonus just for signing up: a promo, foil [Crystalline Sliver](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crystalline+Sliver). Like all the premier events before it (and coming soon!), Grand Prix- Denver is a good time for everyone, even if you’re not playing in the main event.

  

## Round 15 Feature Match – A Commanding Victory - Jarvis Yu vs. Gerry Thompson

*by Nate Price*

After losing the die roll, Gerry, ever the ball of sunshine, started in about how he "never wins die rolls."

"I think I’ve won three in the tournament."

Jarvis’ Kithkin deck started off uncharacteristically slow, not putting a creature into play until his third-turn [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn). Gerry had a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) to stop the potentially 3/3 [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) that Jarvis had behind. With the ground slowed down, Jarvis took to the skies with a [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession), but Gerry brought the world crashing down with a [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout).

After the Spout, Jarvis built back up quickly with a [Stillmoon Cavalier](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stillmoon+Cavalier) and [Knight of Meadowgrain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Meadowgrain). All Gerry had to fill the void was a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter). When Jarvis sent his creatures in, Gerry chumped both creatures, clearing his side of the table. When Jarvis attacked in, Gerry had a [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) (Surprise!) waiting to block the Cavalier. Jarvis had a [Cloudgoat Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudgoat+Ranger) to drop four creatures into play, but Gerry had a [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout), leaving the [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) as the only remaining creature. He added insult to injury with a [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) naming Jarvis’ only potential attacker: [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f4/40/f44027aa7b73a0988fd31ff183966257d0b4e5fb24b00a19c1e74719b91e463c.jpg)*Gerry Thompson wishes looks could kill.*

Jarvis got back on the board with a [Stillmoon Cavalier](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stillmoon+Cavalier), but Gerry trumped it on his turn with an [Archon of Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archon+of+Justice). Jarvis figured he had to do something, and just emptied his hand of [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn) and [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart). The Cavalier was able to hold the Archon off thanks to its protection and ability to gain flying, but the giant [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) was still a monster. After the Cenn sacrificed himself for Jarvis’ life total, Gerry played a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) and passed the turn.

Jarvis drew his card, surveyed the board, and passed the turn. Gerry, not really intimidated by what Jarvis may have held, attacked with his whole team. Jarvis was forced to activate his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), so that he would have three creatures. The Stalwart jumped in front of the Thresher, the Archon ran into the Cavalier again, and the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) got eaten by the [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks). With nothing else to do, Gerry passed the turn with Jervis keeping himself at eight.

Jarvis once again drew his card, looked around the board and passed it to Gerry. During Gerry’s upkeep, he chose to [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake) the [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher). Gerry said "Ok," and removed it from the game. He simply played a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter), and attacked with his finks and Archon. The Cavaliers took a turn off of Archon duty, instead choosing to eat the Finks. This dropped Jarvis to four. Gerry also had a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) to get another [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) into play. This prompted Jarvis to [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake) the [Archon of Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archon+of+Justice), removing it from the game and bypassing the graveyard trigger. When Jarvis tried to play a [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) to buy some more turns, Gerry had the killing blow with a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to counter the Procession and tap the Cavalier.

Thompson 1, Yu 0

"What were your losses to," Jarvis asked between games?

"I lost to Faeries, the mirror match, and my friend Owen," Gerry informed him.

"I really don’t want to play this matchup. I’ve already lost to it once," Jarvis shakily told Gerry. He knows that this matchup isn’t one of the best for his decks.

"Yeah, I’m pretty comfortable with it," Gerry said, grinning with confidence.

Gerry had to go to six cards for the second game, but was rewarded with a [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo), naming the [Knight of Meadowgrain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Meadowgrain) that Jarvis had just played. Jarvis untapped, and sulkily tapped his mana. "Play *another* [Knight of Meadowgrain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Meadowgrain)."

"Yes," Gerry cheered with a smile!

Jarvis extended a bit more with a [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart) and [Burrenton Forge-Tender](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burrenton+Forge-Tender),—two creatures able to attack around the Halo. Gerry just kept playing lands. Jarvis, having to deal some damage to Gerry before he established full control, played a [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession), giving him three fliers. Gerry decided that Jarvis had overextended enough, and used [Austere Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Austere+Command) to kill all creatures. Jarvis filled the void up nicely with a [Cloudgoat Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudgoat+Ranger), which warranted a [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) from Gerry. In the wake of the Spout, Gerry added a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) to his team.

Jarvis managed to plop another four creatures into play with another [Cloudgoat Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudgoat+Ranger) on the following turn. Gerry just shrugged an ok. He played a [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) to match the Ranger, and sent it to Jarvis to do his worst. Apparently, Jarvis’ worst was to attack with his Kithkin tokens and [Unmake](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unmake) the Finks before Gerry could block. After much consideration, Gerry chose to block with the Colossus. Jarvis didn’t have anything; he just wanted to use his [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights) to put a [Burrenton Forge-Tender](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burrenton+Forge-Tender) into play. Gerry played another Kitchen finks and passed the turn back to Jarvis.

Jarvis decided to take to the skies by tapping his two tokens and the Tender to give the Ranger flying. When the flying giant sauntered over, Gerry made a flashy [Plumeveil](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plumeveil) to block. Jarvis sighed and put the Ranger into the graveyard. Jarvis followed that up with a [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) to take to the freshly cleared skies.

The skies were only temporarily clear, though, and Gerry used a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) to return a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to play and block a Spirit on Jarvis’ next attack. Jarvis then added the powerhouse [Oversoul of Dusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oversoul+of+Dusk) to his team. Gerry, unworried about the Oversoul, sent his [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) and Colossus into attack. After the Oversoul killed his Finks and the Colossus killed a Kithkin token, Gerry played [Hallowed Burial](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hallowed+Burial) to reset things.

With the board now cleared, Jarvis took the time to knock Gerry down a peg with his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault). After getting in for two, he filled up the board with a [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart) and a [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn). Gerry played a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) to kill the Cenn, netting himself a blocker in the process. When Jarvis attacked on the following turn with his Stalwart and [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), Gerry threw his [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) in front of the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault). Jarvis wasn’t too keen on trading, though, and used two [Rustic Clachan](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rustic+Clachan)s to reinforce the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), keeping it alive.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ad/2d/ad2d57a7cf400e04d4b33fee165ff3d7f6367e7cfaa221d0b8957a287b1c62c8.jpg)*Jarvis Yu waits for Gerry to clear the board again.*

Gerry played a 4/4 of his own, though it didn’t seem likely that he’d throw his [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) in front of the Vault. When Jarvis attempted a [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny), Gerry used a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to counter it and draw a card. He got in for four on the following turn with his Colossus, and added a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to his team. When Jarvis didn’t have anything on his turn, Gerry used another [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to tap Jarvis’s team and return the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) to his hand. With no opposition, Gerry was free to attack Jarvis and pump his Colossus twice, doing the final sixteen points in one fell swoop.

"Two impossible matches in a row. I can’t imagine a way to win unless you mulligan to four or so."

"Yeah, it’s pretty rough for you. You were doing the right things, though. You couldn’t win by just sitting there," Gerry consoled him.

Gerry Thompson defeats Jarvis Yu 2-0



  

## Quarterfinals – AJ Sacher vs. Kenny Castor

*by Bill Stark*

AJ Sacher is a Chicago transplant who recently moved to Pennsylvania. His opponent in the first round of the Top 8 is Kansas standout Kenny Castor. He was bringing an innovative Merfolk list to battle featuring "splashes" of white, green, and black. Sacher, meanwhile, had brought a relatively stock blue-black Faeries list replete with [Stillmoon Cavalier](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stillmoon+Cavalier)s in the sideboard, one of the breakout cards of the weekend.

AJ won the die roll but his high hopes for that feat were dampened slightly as he opened on a mulligan. Still, he did manage to get a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) going right away, while Castor gained some momentum from a [Stonybrook Banneret](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonybrook+Banneret) to allow him to play more spells for less. One of those spells happened to be [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus), not only a Merfolk but a Wizard too and one of the top creatures in the Block Constructed format. Kenny managed to sneak it onto the board after his opponent had skipped a fourth land drop and was tapped too low to counter.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/04/05/040536d246ca9475a58cf1ffd1c9e126035cf90248f0eb201a3cf989d33721f7.jpg)*Could AJ Sacher overcome his opponent’s Merfolk horde?*Sacher attempted to make a game of it with his [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) and a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite) but without land forthcoming to let him make a solution in the form of [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) or [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation), the young player from Illinois was in a tight spot. Castor meanwhile was all too happy to swing with his Colossus and Banneret, both with a form of evasion against AJ; the 4/4’s protection from black prevented [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom)‘s Faerie Rogues from blocking it while the 1/1 Banneret had good ole’ fashioned islandwalk. It didn’t hurt that [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) was gradually wearing Sacher away as well. When two draw steps failed to yield him the answer he needed, AJ conceded the game.

Castor 1, Sacher 0

"I probably should have gone to five," AJ Sacher opined while he and his opponent quietly shuffled for the second game of their match, indicating his decision to keep his six card hand. Kenny Castor simply nodded and continued shuffling, focusing instead on his first Grand Prix Top 8.

A turn one [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) for AJ Sacher revealed his opponent’s hand to include [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher), [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus), [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation), [Wispmare](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wispmare), and three lands. AJ opted to nab the [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher). A second copy of the *Lorwyn* discard spell for AJ on the turn two revealed Castor had simply drawn a land for his turn, and Sacher took the [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) to make sure his opponent would have a hard time repeating his plan from the first game.

For the second time in the match, however, AJ Sacher missed land drop number four, instead using his mana attacking with a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) and setting the score at 16-14 in his favor. Kenny’s first creature of the game was a [Sower of Temptation](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sower+of+Temptation), failing to steal anything from Sacher. Humorously enough AJ’s plan to answer his opponent’s Sower was to steal it with one of his own, finally hitting his fourth land (and stopping mid-spell to silence his ringing cell phone).

The war of Sowers continued through the distraction as Kenny used a second one to re-steal, but AJ had a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) to leave things in his favor both on the board and in life totals at 16-8. Kenny tried to evoke a [Wispmare](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wispmare) to kill a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) from his opponent, but Sacher opted to counter with [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite). That merited an evoked response from Castor in the form of [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) which wiped both player’s boards. Unfortunately for the tapped out Castor, Sacher had a [Puppeteer Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer+Clique) in his hand which was all too happy to steal Kenny’s [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) and bash for 9 total damage, sending the players to a rubber game.

Castor 1, Sacher 1

Kenny Castor was perfectly fine with his opening hand in the final game of the match, but AJ Sacher wasn’t, opting to ship his hand for a fresh set of six. A first turn [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) from Sacher managed to clear his opponent’s hand of a [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus), but Castor made up for any loss of card advantage by playing a [Silvergill Adept](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Silvergill+Adept). When Sacher played a [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom), Kenny obliged by using a [Wispmare](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wispmare) to kill the troublesome enchantment.

"That’s pretty good," Sacher chided his opponent with a mock frown. The [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) from the beginning of the game had failed to reveal the 1/3 meaning Castor had managed to draw it over the course of just a few turns. Kenny just shrugged and overloaded the board with creatures, adding [Sygg, River Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sygg%2C+River+Guide) and a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) to go alongside his [Wispmare](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wispmare).

Clearly backpedaling, AJ Sacher could do nothing but meekly play a [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite). With the score at 20-4 and desperately needing a miracle to pull him out of his situation, AJ tried to mount a comeback. It didn’t look likely, however, as Castor’s Sygg promised to protect his most prominent threats from any type of removal Sacher could muster as well as giving his creatures evasion to swing in against any possible blockers from AJ. Using a [Consign to Dream](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consign+to+Dream), Sacher made a go of it anyway, attempting to return Kenny’s attacking Sygg to hand. When that failed to resolve and a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) was countered as well, Sacher extended his hand in defeat.

Kenny Castor defeats AJ Sacher 2-1.

  

## Quarterfinals: How Do You Say Mulligan In Italian? – Antonino DeRosa vs. Nathan Elkins

*by Nate Price*

"Is this your first Grand Prix Top 8," DeRosa asked as they shuffled up before the match?

"Yeah," Elkins sheepishly admitted.

Always the good guy, DeRosa congratulated him on his achievement. "Congrats! Hopefully there are many more in your future."

"Ironically, I had just quit the game, but decided I wanted to go to Berlin. I played in a PTQ and won it, and then decided that I might as well play here." This just goes to prove the point I made yesterday: people may take breaks from Magic, but no one ever quits.

When DeRosa won the die roll and chose to go first, Elkins toyed around with him a bit.

"You're sure you don't want the extra card," Elkins asked?

"Nah, I've got [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter)s," DeRosa responded.

"Yeah, but you have to *pay* for those cards," Elkins laughed.

Still confident in his decision, DeRosa drew his opening hand, and was pretty happy with his opening grip. Elkins, on the other hand, couldn't rationalize keeping a one-land [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) hand. His next six were slightly better, though, and he kept.

A first-turn [Goldmeadow Stalwart](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldmeadow+Stalwart) was followed by a second-turn [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) for Elkins. DeRosa got the uber-defensive [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) on his third turn, slowing Elkins down and bolstering his own life total. Elkins was unable to come up with a third land, which was causing him some serious pain. "My hand is the nuts," he cried, "Which I guess usually happens when you don't have any land."

He did find the third land on the following turn, and used his newly found second white source to play a [Knight of Meadowgrain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Meadowgrain). DeRosa, who hadn't stumbled yet in the match, was able to play an [Archon of Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archon+of+Justice) on the following turn. Elkins wasted no time on [Crib Swap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crib+Swap)ping it, getting around the Archon's nasty trigger. DeRosa had some removal of his own, and [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw)ed the [Knight of Meadowgrain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Meadowgrain), leaving Elkins with only the Stalwart he opened with.

On his next turn, Elkins went from zero to sixty in a hurry. He found a fifth land, and quintupled his creature count with a [Cloudgoat Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudgoat+Ranger). DeRosa didn't want things to get too out of control, though, and chose to [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) the board away after attacking with his [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw). His [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) survived the blast, and brought him back up to sixteen life.

Elkins had a [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) on the following turn, providing him with three creatures at the cost of one card. Kithkin decks are incredibly capable of vomiting creatures onto the table at anytime. Elkins sent his new fliers into the red zone on his following attack, which prompted DeRosa to [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) back to block. The attack allowed Elkins to use his [Windbrisk Heights](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Windbrisk+Heights), though, netting him an incredibly cheap [Cloudgoat Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudgoat+Ranger)

DeRosa wasn't without options, though, and used [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) to clear the board after attacking Elkins down to eleven. He followed that up with a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), once again returning himself to sixteen. Elkins had a [Crib Swap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crib+Swap) for the persistent Finks, giving DeRosa a changeling in its stead. He also had a [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn), turning his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) into an attacking 3/3. A second [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) joined the team, really starting to put the screws to DeRosa. When [Ajani Goldmane](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Goldmane) showed up at the party, things started to look grim for the usually jovial DeRosa. Elkins activated his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s and then used Ajani to pump his Vaults and Cenn, and then sent a single [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) into attack.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e6/07/e607da6274bfe6cf6c8e24b45988bf941c092913244e84f0b740d15c3d6a34ed.jpg)*Nathan Elkins just wants DeRosa to run out of Wrath effects. Please?*  
 DeRosa had a [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) to return a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to play, and he used it to drop Ajani to his last counter, which Elkins was more than happy to remove on his next turn to pump his team one last time. When he activated his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s to attack, DeRosa had a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to tap all of Elkins' troops and return one of the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s to his hand.

"This is the weakest Wrath of God ever," DeRosa sighed as he aimed an [Austere Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Austere+Command) at a [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn) and nonexistent enchantments. After the weak Wrath had resolved, Elkins flashed a [Thistledown Liege](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thistledown+Liege) into play. He dropped a [Knight of Meadowgrain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Meadowgrain) into play on his own turn, and sent his attackers in. Before they could turn sideways, though, DeRosa used a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to tap his creatures and return the remaining large [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) to Elkins' hand. When he untapped, DeRosa used a red-only [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) to clear away Elkins' creatures, leaving himself with a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter), and Elkins with only a [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) in his hand.

When Elkins sent his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) in, DeRosa blocked it with his Mannequined [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter), knocking Elkins down to a single [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault). DeRosa replaced the dead [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) with a fresh on on the following turn that prompted Elkins to remark, "Stop drawing cards! It's so good for you!" DeRosa just smiled and played the hammer: [Archon of Justice](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archon+of+Justice). When Elkins tried to get on the board with a [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny), DeRosa aimed another "bad Wrath" at tiny creatures, aka the Figure, and artifacts, aka nothing. Apparently, if Terror were white, it would cost six mana.

It only took a couple of swings for DeRosa's big men to finish the game.

DeRosa 1, Elkins 0

"Well, I guess I'll play," Elkins said before drawing his hand.

Giving him a little of the business he was given before the first game, DeRosa responded with, "That's how you've gotten this far: expert decisions like that."

Elkins played along for fun's sake. "It's what everyone else is doing, so I figure it's got to be right."

The mulligan he was forced into, though, wasn't very much fun. His next six were much better, and he kept without hesitation. On the other side of the table, DeRosa opted to mulligan his opening draw as well, and seemed as pleased as Elkins did with his. When Elkins pressed him about it, he denied that his hand was any good.

"You like it, eh?"

"Nah, not really. I just really didn't want to go to five."

[Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny) provided a literal one-two punch for Elkins as two copies of the powerful *Eventide* rare came down for Elkins. They both leveled up once, and were soon joined by a [Burrenton Forge-Tender](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burrenton+Forge-Tender). Now that his [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout)s weren't an out to the two Figures, DeRosa was backpedaling fast. They Kithkin had DeRosa down to nine before his fourth turn.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/1e/99/1e999614816f22a189643fb720f1cfd111b94e6fcaa5c0e0f66a7c395f43f60c.jpg)*Antonino DeRosa has some ground to make up.*

Elkins' Forge-Tender couldn't stop the double evoked [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw)s that DeRosa had ready for the two Figures, and the vicious little Kithkin joined the [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw)s in the graveyard. Elkins switched modes at this point, and used a [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) to take to the skies. His Spirits knocked DeRosa down to five. A second Procession followed the attack, which threatened lethal damage, but left Elkins vulnerable to [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher) or [Austere Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Austere+Command). DeRosa had neither, and he scooped up his cards before Elkins could kill him.

DeRosa 1, Elkins 1

After mulliganing his opening draw, DeRosa thought for a good fifteen seconds about his second six cards before finally deciding to keep. Things got a little better as Elkins mulliganed for the third consecutive game. While he was shuffling his deck, Elkins asked DeRosa if there was an Italian word for mulligan. When DeRosa didn't have one, he offered his own take on it using some actual Italian. DeRosa, who lived in Italy for nine years or so was intrigued.

"How do you know Italian," he asked?

"I lived in Italy for two years," Elkins told him.

They discussed places they'd lived and seemed to have a pretty good time reminiscing about their respective times there.

Goldmeadow stalwart made another appearance for Elkins, and he was joined the turn after by a [Figure of Destiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Figure+of+Destiny). DeRosa had a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) to kill the Figure, but didn't have a third land. When Elkins played a second figure, but no third land of his own, DeRosa laughed, "two handicapped decks going at it!"

"My deck is a little more efficient on two lands," Elkins argued with no resistance from DeRosa.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/76/8a/768a1897e513fe669350e20dc3725d35bef605c78661387ab48d385be7cad729.jpg)*The real question is 'Why is that orange crying?'*

DeRosa began bargaining with his deck. "Come on, just one more land. I'll even take a tapped vivid land!" Elkins found a third land, and dropped a [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn) into play, but not before DeRosa got to [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions) it. Elkins had the mana to pay it, but it did allow DeRosa to clash. He cheered when his clash card was a [Fire-Lit Thicket](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fire-Lit+Thicket). Elkins' attack dropped DeRosa to eleven, but DeRosa's third land allowed him to [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) the board clear.

Despite just having lost three creatures, Elkins replaced them with a [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession). He also added a [Burrenton Forge-Tender](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burrenton+Forge-Tender) to his team, preventing any more [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout)s. DeRosa had to [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) back into play to kill the Forge-Tender before he could [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) the turn after. It cleared the fliers away, although not before they knocked him down to five.

Almost on cue, Elkins replaced his dead creatures with a [Cloudgoat Ranger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudgoat+Ranger), which got [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout)ed away by DeRosa. When another Ranger hit play, DeRosa had an [Austere Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Austere+Command) to wipe the slate clean once again. A [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) managed to find a chance to attack, and dropped DeRosa to three. Elkins also found a [Knight of Meadowgrain](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Meadowgrain). However, DeRosa had the perfect answer in [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks). They brought him back up to five and provided a blocker. As is becoming routine in this match, Elkins had the perfect answer with a [Crib Swap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crib+Swap) to completely remove the Finks. The Changeling token got to block the Knight for a turn, although the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault) dropped him to three.

When Elkins found a second [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault), and DeRosa had no play, it seemed like the game was over. Elkins played a [Wizened Cenn](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wizened+Cenn) before activating his [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s. Before he could attack with them, though, DeRosa used [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) to tap all of Elkins' creatures and draw a card. DeRosa played a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter), drawing himself a couple of cards before passing the turn with four mana available. With four mana available and a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) in the graveyard, Elkins had to consider both [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) and [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin) as possibilities from DeRosa. Eventually, he chose to activate both [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s and attack. DeRosa squinted for a second, but didn't actually have anything and dropped his hand on the table in concession.

Nathan Elkins defeats Antonino DeRosa 2-1

  

## Quarterfinals: Lee Steht vs Hunter Burton

*by Riki Hayashi*

In a Top 8 with some big names, this is the matchup between the unknowns, at least as players. Lee Steht is the co-host of the ManaNation podcast that coverage reporter Bill Stark said he watched on his flight into Denver. He also "taught Antonino how to play when he was 10." Sitting next to his old friend, De Rosa added, "Then the apprentice became the master."

![](https://i.ormos.uk/89/e5/89e525d71b18aac195bd14ff3532c0383aa97ec6784632c2ee788694ffd23b65.jpg)

Hunter Burton drove from Texas although he admitted that he "slept most of the way," leaving the driving to his friends.

Looking over each other's decklists, both players agreed that [Puppeteer Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer+Clique), a rather unusual maindeck choice for Lee, was poised to play an important role in the matchup. Lee then told one of those "too good to be true" stories about Puppeteer Cliquing a [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher), which killed the Clique, then getting back a second Thresher for the full 18 point swing.

Things got serious and both players shuffled quietly after the initial banter. That was until Lee caught a fly in his hand and everyone had a good Mr. Miyagi-related chuckle. Hunter won the roll, but sent back his first hand. "The only way I can win this matchup," said Lee, as he shuffled Hunter's deck after the mull. The voodoo didn't work. Hunter kept his six, but admitted "This hand's pretty awful."

Lee opened with a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), further setting Hunter back by taking the early action in his hand. [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) hit the graveyard, and Hunter was left with a slow hand with [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) and a pair of [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command)s.

Both players did the back and forth with lands until Hunter loaded up with a [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus). Lee "matched" it with [Scion of Oona](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scion+of+Oona). It looked like Lee would have to take a big hit from the Colossus, but he responded to the pump ability with [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) in bounce-and-draw mode. When Hunter tried to get the Colossus back into play the following turn, Lee animated two [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s and countered with [Spellstutter Sprite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellstutter+Sprite). Insult to injury was the aforementioned [Puppeteer Clique](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puppeteer+Clique) stealing [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus) and removing it from the game to keep it from getting Profaned back.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ff/a4/ffa41b24a0003b50231c2d7efb08e1afe4ee63eaba9b7853a25295d0ebe0c324.jpg)

Hunter rebuilt with [Doran, the Siege Tower](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doran%2C+the+Siege+Tower) and a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion) on the Scion, but his next play of [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command) for 4 met with another case of the [Mutavault](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutavault)s and Spellstutters. That plus a Vendillion Clique got Hunter to scoop up his cards.

Steht 1, Burton 0

Lee sideboarded and showed me what he was bringing in: [Stillmoon Cavalier](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stillmoon+Cavalier). The card has skyrocketed in popularity between Nationals and Grand Prix- Denver putting them on the metagame map.

Steht started off with another turn one [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), stifling Hunter's early game by taking [Wren's Run Vanquisher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wren%27s+Run+Vanquisher). [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) and [Stillmoon Cavalier](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stillmoon+Cavalier) constituted Lee's early army, while Hunter played the protection from black [Chameleon Colossus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chameleon+Colossus). But the second verse was the same as the first, as Lee had a bounce spell, [Consign to Dream](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consign+to+Dream)s this time, in response to the pump ability.

Hunter tried to recover from the tempo loss with [Treefolk Harbinger](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treefolk+Harbinger) for Doran. [Bitterblossom](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bitterblossom) kept spitting out tokens and gave Lee enough Faeries to Spellstutter the Siege Tower. Hunter tried to get Doran back with [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command), but a counter-and-draw [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) got Hunter to shrug and say "You win."

Lee Steht defeats Hunter Burton 2-0.

Speaking about the match afterwards, Lee said, "Thoughseize was so helpful... Faeries is just so good... so good."

  

## Quarterfinals - Gerry Thompson vs. Kyle Bundgaard

*by Steve Sadin*

Gerry Thompson, who is playing in his 6th Grand Prix Top 8, brought a Quick 'N Toast variant designed by Patrick Chapin that was piloted by many of the top American **Magic** players including Paul Cheon, Luis Scot-Vargas, Brandon Scheel, Patrick Chapin and Sam Black. Of these players, only Gerry was able to advance to the elimination rounds.

Kyle, the only Colorado native to make it to the Top 8, brought a 5cControl list of his own creation.

Gerry: "My only losses so far have been punts."  
 Kyle: "My only loss was to Faeries."

Needless to say, both players were happy with their deck choices.

"How many lands are you running?" Gerry asked his opponent.

"24, you?"

"Only 24 lands, man, I have 26. I love lands, I wish I had ran a 27th."

Gerry won the die-roll and they were off. Off to a lightning fast 5-color control mirror...

Gerry mulliganed down to 6 while Kyle kept his opener. Thompson was first on the board with a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) which Kyle [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion)ed. On his turn, Kyle evoked a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter).

Gerry attacked with his 2/1 Finks putting Kyle on 18, then played a second [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks). Kyle played a turn four [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers), killing one of Gerry's [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) and making the other a 2/1. Gerry played a fifth land and attacked with his Finks, which Kyle blocked. This left both players with an empty board.

At the end of Kyle's sixth turn, Gerry made a [Cloudthresher](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cloudthresher), which Kyle countered with [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command). Thompson untapped and played [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter). Kyle then cast a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), making the life scores 20 for Kyle and 26 for Gerry. Kyle then [Makeshift Mannequin](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Makeshift+Mannequin)ed back his [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter).

Gerry passed his turn, and traded [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter)s with Kyle. Kyle played a second [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), which Gerry attempted to counter with [Broken Ambitions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Broken+Ambitions). Kyle countered Gerry's counter with a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), choosing to bounce one of Gerry's Vivid lands with the second mode on the four-mana instant.

Using a [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) Gerry made his opponent's Finks a 2/1 and then passed the turn back to Kyle. Bundgaard used the opportunity to attack with both of his [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) and Gerry blocked the 3/2, leaving Kyle with two 2/1 Finks. The attack left Gerry at 21 and Kyle at 26.

Thompson cast an [Austere Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Austere+Command) to wipe the board clean, but Kyle followed up strongly with [Oona, Queen of the Fae](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oona%2C+Queen+of+the+Fae). Not willing to be outdone, Gerry played [Runed Halo](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Runed+Halo) naming the 5/5.

When Kyle made his third [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) of the game it looked like he was about to pull away, but Gerry cast an end-of-turn [Oona's Grace](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oona%27s+Grace) to ensure that he would draw spells for the rest of the game. On his own turn, Gerry cast a [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) and a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks).

Kyle attacked with his [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) and Gerry blocked. After combat, Kyle cast [Profane Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Profane+Command) dealing 9 to Gerry and returning [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) to play. This left the life totals at 26 for Kyle and 14 for Gerry. Looking to deal with the 2/2 flier, Gerry played a [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) for 2G to kill Kyle's [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter). Kyle cast a [Soul Snuffers](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Soul+Snuffers), making Gerry's [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) a 2/1.

Gerry cast another [Mulldrifter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulldrifter) and at the end of Gerry's turn, Kyle killed Gerry's [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) with a [Nameless Inversion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170727040336/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nameless+Inversion).
